#7052. AI Native Creative Strategist - EdTech (French fluent 🇫🇷) at Paradox

What if your next playground was to build a global, iconic Brand, with a creative machine that never sleeps, and compounds?

We’re not looking for a "Social Media Manager" to follow a calendar. We’re looking for an AI-native Creative Strategist (Brand & Content).

Someone who understands that to win today, you don’t just need "content", you need a Movement.

This isn’t another content job. Most content teams use AI to write captions faster. That’s the 99%. We want to be in the 0.1%: a media where AI runs the creative loop, from the signal to the hook, the cut, the post and the lesson. Every day, on every platform, in French and in English. You bring the taste. The machine you build brings the scale.

Here’s the bet: a company that learns by itself grows in a way a company of people alone can’t. We aim for $10M in revenue with 10 people, each of them running a dozen agents.

Paradoxically, the more AI runs our content, the more human it has to feel. AI doesn’t make more of what everyone makes. It makes more of what only we can make.

1. Read the signals. Comments, DMs, retention graphs, saves, shares, search trends, objections from sales calls, and what the best creators and advertisers in the world are publishing right now.

2. Hold the full context of Paradox. Our DNA, years of interviews, lives and courses, member stories, and every past post with what it taught us, in one shared company brain.

3. Generate in our voice. Hooks, scripts, shorts, carousels, cuts, thumbnails and captions, in French and in English.

4. Ship. Format, schedule, publish and answer, on every platform.

5. Measure with real KPIs. Retention, shares, saves, follower growth, and the followers who become leads and members. Not likes.

6. Learn and loop on its own. Double down on what works, kill what doesn’t, write the lesson back into the brain, and start the next cycle without waiting for a meeting.

This is already real. An ads library of thousands of creatives feeds our creative research. Agents cut ads from long-form content and wire Instagram comment-to-DM automations. And our product is AI-native too: Athena, our AI coach, works with members every day.

At Paradox, we are convinced that greatness is trainable, and we believe self-help is broken.

Most learning platforms are used the same way: consuming generic theories, getting quick highs, ending with no real change.

People deserve more: a system that knows them, one that adapts, challenges, and grows with them.

That’s why we want to empower people at scale, by leveraging cutting edge technologies like AI, and crafting user experiences with outstanding aesthetics.

- Human Intelligence (coaches, peers, and mentors)

- Structured Intelligence (protocols, mental models, rituals)

- Technological Intelligence (AI personalization, feedback loops, adaptive journeys)

To create one seamless ecosystem: Paradox OS.

Paradox was founded by David Laroche, a creator followed by over 2 million people and known for his interviews with icons like Tony Parker, Mike Horn, Richard Branson and Gad Elmaleh. We started in French. We’re building for the world.

You lead the Content Factory not by management, but with creativity.

In a classic content team, people do the work and AI helps. Here it’s the other way round: agents do the volume, you design the machine, set the bar and make the calls. Humans do what no machine can: taste.

Build a symbiotic strategy where David Laroche’s personal brand and Paradox’s company brand reinforce each other without cannibalization.

Implement radical new formats (documentaries, conceptual shorts) that shatter the boring codes of the "Self-Help" industry.

Define distinct, sharp editorial lines for each platform: Depth & Search for YouTube, Aesthetic & Vibe for Instagram, and Authority & Authenticity for LinkedIn.

❌ The old way: a strategy deck written once a year, and a calendar filled by habit.

🏴‍☠️ At Paradox: agents watch every platform, every competitor and every comment continuously. They tell you which angle is rising, which format is tiring and which story our audience asks for. The strategy updates every week, on evidence.

Take responsibility for the entire production chain from hooks to storyboarding the narrative, and ensuring the final cut matches the vision.

Own the packaging: titles, thumbnails and the first 3 seconds. You test variants every week and know your click-through and retention numbers by heart.

❌ The old way: one shoot becomes one video and a handful of posts.

🏴‍☠️ At Paradox: one shoot, one live or one interview becomes dozens of hooks, shorts, carousels and ad cuts by the next morning, in both languages. Concepts are tested with AI-generated drafts before we spend a day shooting them.

Your taste becomes the eval. You write down what makes a great hook, a great cut, a great carousel and a great story, in rules and examples the agents check themselves against. Each time you reject something, the reason goes back into the rules.

Act as the final quality check: nothing gets published if it doesn’t meet the "Paradox Standard" of aesthetics and storytelling, whether a person or an agent made it.

❌ The old way: a creative team briefs an editor, waits three days and gets two versions.

🏴‍☠️ At Paradox: agents draft, cut, caption, translate and schedule. You keep the best, and next week their first drafts are already closer to your bar.

Analyze retention graphs, CTR, and CPL to iterate based on feedback, killing what fails and aggressively scaling what works.

❌ The old way: someone pulls the stats at the end of the month and nobody changes anything.

🏴‍☠️ At Paradox: agents read the numbers of every post every day, explain why it worked or didn’t, and propose the next test. You measure the machine itself: posts per week, time from idea to live, hit rate, and the share of the work done without a human.

We started in French. You build the machine that takes Paradox to English-speaking audiences: channels, formats and voice that feel native, not translated.

And always: real stories told with consent, no fake claims, no fake scarcity. The machine can scale, but it doesn’t decide what’s right. You do.

How you’ll know you’re AI-native: you spend your time on concepts, taste and calls, not on resizing, captioning and exporting. The output grows every month while the team doesn’t.

Proven Content Builder: You have successfully grown a media brand, a YouTube channel, or a personal brand to significant scale (100k+ followers or millions of views). You understand the mechanics of virality and retention. You’ve created at least one format that broke out, and you can show its click-through and retention numbers.

Elite Scripting Skills: You can show us scripts you wrote that performed. You know how to structure a narrative, write a hook that stops the scroll, and keep an audience engaged for 20+ minutes.

Performance Fluency: You have worked in environments where content had to drive business results (ads, launches, conversion), not just vanity metrics.

AI-native Creative: you’ve built content pipelines with AI yourself: Claude Code, automations, generative video, image and voice tools, evals. Show us something you built, not a certificate.

Intolerant to Mediocrity: You have "Taste." You are physically pained by a bad font, a clumsy cut, or a weak headline. You fight for the details that others ignore, including in what AI produces.

Psychology Obsessed: You don’t just "make content"; you study human nature. You constantly ask why people click, why they share, and what emotion drives them.

A "Doer" with Authority: You command respect not by title, but by competence. You can jump into a project, write the script, direct the shoot, and fix the edit if needed.

Loop thinker: you see every platform as a loop that can learn, and you can’t stop yourself from closing it.

No Ego, Just Impact: You don’t fall in love with your ideas. You accept that even the best concepts can flop. When it happens, you don’t take it personally; you analyze the data, pivot immediately, and ship the next concept with renewed energy.

English: Fluent English, you can hold a conversation on specific details with English-speaking colleagues.

Our chosen language for written communication is English.

❌ If you need clarity before chaos: wrong place.

❌ If you prefer balance over intensity: wrong season.

❌ If you don’t genuinely care about the topics we cover: wrong place.

❌ If you want to strategize without getting your hands dirty in scripting, directing, or the editing suite: wrong role.

❌ If you think AI content means generic content: wrong role. We use AI to multiply what only we can make.

❌ If you want to manage a large team: we’d rather build the machine than grow the headcount.

Help us market a product that fuses neuroscience, design, and AI to reinvent how humans grow.

Build one of the first truly AI-native media, and write the playbook others will copy.

Work with filmmakers, psychologists, engineers, and creatives obsessed with impact.

You’ll compete with San Francisco companies.

-> 🏅 Being surrounded by A-players who strive to perform

But you don’t just want a competitive paycheck, you also want…

-> Playing a crucial role in the development of a company whose content and products will be used by millions of people

-> Waking up in the morning feeling like you are making a difference is something!

We receive messages every day from clients who have successfully lost weight, quit smoking, reached a milestone in their business, built better relationships, and made positive changes in their lives.

But that’s not all, we believe that driven people deserve…

-> 😎 A company culture based on autonomy & accountability

-> 🗽 Flexible working hours (but very hard working)

-> 🌎 A remote culture from day one with the freedom to work from wherever you want

-> 🧍‍♀️ Standing desks paid by the company if you live far from our offices

-> 🏀 350€ per year paid by the company to take care of yourself (for your gym, or dance class or whatever make you feel great)

-> 📚 150€ of books or education - At Paradox, we are learner lovers and we want to support you in that

-> 🔥 Access to Athena - our Paradox AI Coach supporting you on anything personal or professional, we want you to live your best life

⚠️ To complete the recruitment process, each candidate must organize calls with his or her previous managers. If you are not comfortable with this : do not apply ⚠️

If all this sounds good to you, come and write the next chapter with us 🚀🚀🚀🚀

📃 A 6 to 8 minutes application survey, with links to content you made and something you built with AI

💻 A 30mn video call with Timy, HR, to deep dive into your career path and ambitions

💻 A 1h video call, to discuss how you’d fit into Paradox’s content team

💻 A 1h culture-fit video call, to assess alignment with our values and mindset

💻 A 1h final video call with David, CEO, to discuss the creative vision and the next milestones to build

💼 Reference check to understand how you operate across environments and help set you up for a smooth onboarding

Our recruitment process is tailored to do our best to avoid that you or us realize that we’re not a perfect fit. Every step serves a purpose and will allow us to get to know you better as much as it’ll help you understand more where you’ll be stepping in.

We also want you to have time to ask all of your questions regarding the mission, the long-term vision of your team and the company, our values, with the relevant interlocutor in front of you.

If you’re here to build a media that changes how millions of people grow.

Let’s build the Operating System for Greatness.

New remote jobs delivered to your inbox, every week.

Location independence is the future of work. It serves companies with best talent from around the globe. And benefits employees with a greater work life balance and the ability to work in their pyjamas.- Nithur, founder.

The problem isn’t that teachers don’t want to learn, but that professional development is treated as an event, rather than an ongoing process.

#7044. Mercury: Head of Product - Business Banking

In 1851, a New York bookkeeper named George D. Lyman observed the same absurd ritual each afternoon: porters carried bags of coins and bundles of checks bank to bank down Wall Street, settling by hand, a fortune exposed to error and theft. Lyman's fix: send everything through one central office and settle the net. On October 11, 1853, in the basement of 14 Wall Street, 52 banks cleared $22.6 million in a single sitting. Money between banks had stopped being an event and became seamless infrastructure.

A century and a half later, the hard part is unchanged: moving money once is easy; making it work every time, for hundreds of thousands of businesses, across every rail, is hard. The bank account is where a company's financial life happens - payroll clears or it doesn't; a wire lands or a deal slips; the month closes clean, or someone loses a weekend to it. Mercury is building a business banking* product at a scale better than anything else in the market.

Business Banking is the heart of Mercury. We're looking for a Head of Product to own it end-to-end: setting product strategy, leading a team of product managers, driving deposit and payment growth, and owning business performance.

*Mercury is a fintech company, not an FDIC-insured bank. Banking services provided through Choice Financial Group and Column N.A., Members FDIC.

Mercury values diversity & belonging and is proud to be an Equal Employment Opportunity employer. All individuals seeking employment at Mercury are considered without regard to race, color, religion, national origin, age, sex, marital status, ancestry, physical or mental disability, veteran status, gender identity, sexual orientation, or any other legally protected characteristic. We are committed to providing reasonable accommodations throughout the recruitment process for applicants with disabilities or special needs. If you need assistance, or an accommodation, please let your recruiter know once you are contacted about a role.

Total RewardsThe total rewards package at Mercury includes base salary, equity (stock options/RSUs), and benefits.

Our salary and equity ranges are highly competitive within the SaaS and fintech industry and are updated regularly using the most reliable compensation survey data for our industry. New hire offers are made based on a candidate’s experience, expertise, geographic location, and internal pay equity relative to peers.

Our target new hire base salary ranges for this role are the following:

No forms. Just smart, optimized applications.

#7043. I Am the Wildest Dream of Every Black and Brown Trans Woman Before Me

And it is my duty to honor their legacy while making a way for the next generation of trans girls, just like our luminaries did for me.

As an influencer, a lot of times I'm the only trans person—or the only trans person of color—in the room. It’s a lot of pressure, and there have been times where I have anxiety before going to certain spaces because I will question "Do I belong here?" But I’ve learned that that’s just the patriarchy and society trying to make us feel like we're not worthy.

That’s especially true in this current political and cultural climate. The trans community, especially the Black and brown trans community, gets so much hate—that’s why I’m very offended when people say being trans “is a choice.” Like, living with and navigating these stigmas and mistreatments isn't something that someone would willing choose.

It’s been particularly challenging, too, as a trans person in the public eye; there's been many instances where my safety has been jeopardized. When I get looked at, I'm thinking, They're looking at me either because I'm pretty or because I'm a viral trans person online. The differentiator is literally if I'm being chased home, which has happened multiple times. Since I speak out for trans women that can't speak for themselves, I'm also putting myself at risk. But at the same time, I think I can also make it safer for the next girl down the line.

It's so rewarding being that person others can look up to. I love reassuring other trans women that they can do certain things or just live their lives authentically, loud, and open. But sometimes I'm like, Oh my god, how nice and easy would it be for me to just be silent and be in the background? But I don't think I was born to be a background girl. I'm front and center.

And I think that the trans women before me would want me to be. The hard work that those women put for girls like me now, I have to show up for that. I think about trans women on the front lines of trans rights, like Marsha P. Johnson or Sylvia Rivera—they were front and center at Stonewall. They were the voices for many then, and there was so much more violence. The community was pretty much hidden at the time, too.

There's so much trans history lost, and Trans Day of Remembrance resurfaces those stories. A lot of times things get pushed under the rug because trans people are seen as not important to the media, or people in general, especially after all the propaganda that's been pushed out by certain administrations. But every year on this day, I learn about a different girl, especially outside of the U.S. I’ve learned so much about how some surgeries started because certain trans women needed gender-affirming care, and they paved the way for girls down the line.

Education is the number one thing. I want people to know that your understanding of trans identities is not what you think and that there's a lot more to learn. There are times when I get frustrated because I see the stigmas and the rhetoric online that's really just misinformation, rumors, and lies that people make up about trans people. A lot of trans women have died because that ignorance can lead to trans violence. It's really sad to see. But we made it back then—and it was hard then—so we'll make it now. We're a strong community.

The trans community, we show up for ours (for the most part). There's a couple spaces made for Black trans queer people, like the Black Trans Kitchen by Queen Jean—it’s really beautiful. And Gia Love created the annual Celebration of Black Trans Women Cookout. Trans women that make spaces like that really inspire me because they remind our community that we matter. And we as trans people need spaces where we can just be ourselves, and really show care to one another. Those spaces not only keep me humble, but also remind me of everything I've been through. It makes me feel so grateful and privileged to be alive today because a lot of girls don't make it.

With the work that I do and everything I create, I want other trans women to see me and know that they belong and that they can be in those spaces too. I want to see more, especially more trans women of color, in positions of power and in rooms where you rarely ever see them.

I hope other trans women see that they can be their most authentic self, and that they can feel confident regardless of what anybody says. If you believe you're an amazing, beautiful person, it will reflect on the outside. I want people to feel that way and do that inner work because it's important, especially for our survival as trans people.

Your Horoscope for the Week of November 22

8 Advent Calendars That Aren’t a Total Scam

We're also embracing AI thoughtfully — both in how we build and how we hire to ensure our team is adaptable, innovative, and ready for what's next.

We’re looking for a senior product designer to be the first dedicated designer on our product growth team, shaping how creators get set up, see value in their first days, and enjoy a seamless upgrade experience. Kit ships fast, and the product growth team builds the frameworks and systems that connect creators to the value in their plan as the product keeps changing. We need a designer who can balance rapid experimentation with long-term value for our creators.

Product growth is a new team at Kit, formed earlier this year. In the six months since, we’ve found our rhythm and started to move the numbers, but we’re still shaping how we work, how we prioritise and where we focus in the long term. As the team’s designer, you’ll be the design voice for growth, with real influence over how the team works and what it builds.

You'll own the design of the moments that move our funnel: onboarding, activation, free trials, upgrade and upsell flows, pricing and checkout, and billing. You'll work in a team that learns by shipping, through A/B tests and ship-and-measure changes, and you'll use data to decide what to try next.

This role looks beyond the team. You’ll keep a pulse on what other product teams are shipping and how marketing communicates, so that what creators are promised before sign up matches what they find during onboarding. We’re looking for someone who enjoys looking across Kit to see what's changing, then focusing on what it means for their own work.

This is a full-time IC role for a designer who loves connecting design decisions to numbers, moves quickly without sacrificing quality, and is excited about using AI to accelerate outcomes.

We need someone who thinks deeply about user behaviour, interaction design and the product experience, and who is proactive about finding the next opportunity rather than waiting for a spec.

You'll report to Dan Winer, Director of Product Design, and join a small, high-impact product design team currently consisting of four senior product designers and a senior design engineer. From day one, you’ll be part of the product growth team’s product trio alongside the product manager and engineering manager, shaping the team’s vision, priorities and how we work. You’ll also work closely with the team’s engineers.

You’ll work closely with product marketing on how we talk to creators before and after they sign up, and with the other product designers to stay ahead of what’s shipping across Kit. You’ll also partner with data and our creator success teams.

First week: Get to know Kit’s product and the product growth team’s goals. Get access to Figma, Linear, Mixpanel and our other reporting tools. Take part in get-to-know-you calls with the team.

First month: Get up to speed on how we track and measure creator behaviour in Mixpanel, Omni and Clarity, which experiments are running now, and what we’ve learnt from past ones. Join the product trio’s planning and prioritisation discussions, and ship a small experiment or two, with support from the Director of Product Design as you take ownership of the growth work.

First six months: Own design across the team’s work, which spans growth (onboarding, activation, trials and upgrades) and billing (checkout, plan changes, downgrades and cancellation). This might include personalising onboarding, improving free-to-paid conversion or simplifying our payment flows. By six months, the team should rely on you for a clear picture of how creators move through these flows, where they get stuck and why. You’ll help the team run more and better experiments, from forming a hypothesis to reading the results. We don’t have dedicated researchers, so you’ll talk to creators, run usability tests, track the impact of your work and proactively suggest iterations.

First year: Help the team improve our activation, free-to-paid, trial conversion, and early retention rates, so that more creators upgrade, stay on Kit, and find real value. Turn our upsell, discount and plan change moments into one coherent experience instead of a set of one-off prompts.

Interaction design for complex web applications, with a strong eye for visual hierarchy and detail.

#7032. Mercury: Head of Product - Personal Banking

In 1967, John Shepherd-Barron reached his bank minutes after closing and could not get at his own money. In the bath that evening, he thought of a chocolate bar dispenser and swapped the chocolate for cash. On 27 June, outside a Barclays branch on Enfield High Street, an actor named Reg Varney drew £10 from the world's first cash machine. It took a four-digit code (originally six, until his wife told him nobody remembers six). The bank's hours had stopped being the customer's problem.

Nearly sixty years later, the bar has moved, but the test hasn't: a personal financial product is judged on whether it fits the life of the person using it. People do not read documentation, forgive a confusing screen, or care how the ledger works. They want one place that makes sense of everything — spending, saving, planning, investing — and that anticipates rather than reports: where they stand, what deserves attention, what to do next. Mercury is building personal banking* to that standard.

Personal Banking is how Mercury earns a place in someone's financial life. We're looking for a Head of Product to own it end-to-end: setting product strategy, leading a team of product managers, driving growth and engagement, and owning business performance.

*Mercury is a fintech company, not an FDIC-insured bank. Banking services provided through Choice Financial Group and Column N.A., Members FDIC.

#7024. Designers Say "Cook's Tables" Are About to Replace Kitchen Islands in 2027

Their versatility is just part of their appeal.

Pictured Above: A kitchen designed by Amy Sklar.

For years, oversized kitchen islands have been the be-all and end-all, but lately, designers and homeowners are gravitating toward something a bit smaller: a cook’s table.

Unlike built-in islands that can sometimes dominate a kitchen and cover most of the available floor space, cook’s tables bring a lot of flexibility to the room. Because they’re freestanding, you can move them around depending on your needs (especially if they’re on wheels), whether that’s for prepping meals, hanging out with your morning coffee, or helping kids with homework after school. They also tend to be narrower, making them the perfect solution for small kitchens.

The other benefit is the big charm factor they add. They’re often wooden, with traditional-looking design elements, like turned legs and curvy silhouettes. Cooks tables can also frequently be found in vintage and antique stores, so they’re a great way to work a bit of charm and patina into an otherwise utilitarian space.

As kitchens move away from ultra-sleek and minimal, pieces with warmth and character are becoming more and more popular, and a cook’s table is the perfect example of that. If you’re considering adding one to your own home, learn what to look for—and how to style it—below.

One of the biggest reasons cook’s tables are trending right now is that they soften the overall look and feel of a kitchen. “A traditional island can dominate a kitchen,” says designer Amy Sklar. Because cook’s tables don’t have a bulky built-in base, they feel visually lighter and less imposing, especially in kitchens where space is limited.

Designers also point to the emotional shift happening in homes post-pandemic. “The pandemic reshaped the way we live at home, placing greater value on spaces designed for connection and togetherness,” says designer Becky Ducsik of The Phinery. “Nowhere is this more apparent than in the kitchen, where homeowners are reimagining how they gather and entertain.”

Unlike traditional islands that are packed with appliances and storage, cook’s tables create a more communal focal point centered around gathering and spending time together. That relaxed atmosphere is part of what makes them feel so appealing. Designer Cynthia Lananh notes that cook’s tables strike a balance between practicality and personality, functioning as prep space, dining space, and entertaining hub all at once. “I love a kitchen island that looks like a piece of furniture,” she says, adding that the look feels more casual, approachable, and timeless than a standard built-in island.

While cook’s tables can absolutely make a statement in a large kitchen, designers say they’re especially effective in kitchens with tighter footprints or galley-style layouts. Because they take up less visual and physical space than a traditional island, they can help smaller kitchens feel more open and airy.

That said, larger kitchens can benefit from them too, just in a different way. “With the right vintage find or custom-crafted piece, it can anchor the room and become a striking centerpiece that adds both function and timeless appeal,” says Ducsik.

She recommends using cook’s tables as the focal point in kitchens where storage and appliances can be concentrated along the perimeter. Since seating typically wraps around all sides, she suggests leaving at least four to five feet of clearance around the table to maintain a comfortable flow.

For homeowners who want the look but don’t have space for a freestanding table in the center of the room, Ducsik also suggests integrating one as a peninsula extension at the end of cabinetry for a similar communal feel.

Unlike built-in islands, cook’s tables offer a level of flexibility that’s hard to beat. Since they’re freestanding, they can be moved or adapted more easily over time—a feature designers say is especially useful for entertaining.

Sklar points out that cook’s tables can create a flexible landing zone within a kitchen and are ideal for holidays or large gatherings when extra room is needed. “This is perfect for holidays and large gatherings where you need even more space,” Lananh says. She recommends adding casters if the table will double as both a prep and dining space, making it easier to move around as needed.

Scale is one of the most important things to get right: The table should feel proportional to the room while still leaving enough space to comfortably move around it. Height matters, too. Sklar notes that many vintage tables sit lower than standard counter height, so homeowners may need to modify them slightly with casters or leg extenders to make them more functional for prep work.

As for materials, wood remains the go-to choice for warmth and character. “Durable woods (like oak, maple, or walnut) are popular for their warmth, strength, and character,” says Lananh. “Reclaimed wood adds rustic charm, whereas metal or butcher block tops offer a more durable, industrial aesthetic.”

Ducsik echoes those sentiments and suggests echoing the table’s wood tones throughout the kitchen with details like open shelving, cutting boards, or serving pieces to create a cohesive look.

Lastly, focus on shape. Softer corners and curves can make the space feel more intimate, while rectangular tables can maximize seating and prep space. Ultimately, the goal is for the piece to feel like furniture, not cabinetry. And that’s exactly what makes cook’s tables so appealing in the first place.

A great Product only matters if customers understand its value.

This role will help Uscreen get closer to our customers, tell a clearer Product story, build smarter pricing and packaging, and turn what we build into measurable business growth.

#7002. Your Horoscope for the Week of November 22

After a rather quiet horoscope last week, this week brings lots of cosmic action. First, on Sunday, Sagittarius season begins, changing Scorpio's scheming energy for a more playful, impulsive tone. This will last for the next month and affect Sagittarius placements the most, as well as fellow fire signs Aries and Leo.

Also this week: On Tuesday, a Full Moon in Gemini brings a moment of culmination when it comes to communication. The next day, Mars, the planet of desire and action, moves from exuberant Leo into careful Virgo. For the rest of the year into February 2027, this transit encourages you to stop and think things over before acting, approaching conflict with a plan.

Read your Sun/Rising sign horoscope for the week ahead:

Get out there, Aries. Sagittarius season begins on Sunday and asks you to spend the next month exploring—both literally, through travel (even if it's only a day trip to a nearby town), or metaphorically, through learning something new. On Tuesday, a Full Moon in Gemini asks you to share a truth with someone who needs to hear it. And on Wednesday, Mars moves into Virgo and helps you get your day-to-day schedule in order.

Meet the new Taurus. Sagittarius season helps you discover a whole new you, so spend the next few weeks thinking about—and working towards—who you want to become. Tuesday's Full Moon in Gemini shines on your chart's financial sector, helping you come to a realization about your money habits. And on Wednesday, Mars steps into Virgo and brings order to your creativity. Now's the time to take that great idea you have and turn it into something real.

Turn date night into date month, Gemini. Sagittarius season begins on Sunday and encourages you to focus on your "other half," whether that's a romantic partner, best friend, or creative collaborator. On Tuesday, the annual Full Moon in your sign asks you to celebrate how far you've come since last year. Then, on Wednesday, Mars moves into Virgo, helping you approach any family conflict from a calm, cool, and collected place.

Be well, Cancer. The holidays are often a time for indulging, but Sagittarius season begins on Sunday and reminds you to focus on your health. Meaning that maybe you should stick to one glass of wine at Thanksgiving instead of a whole bottle. On Tuesday, the Full Moon in Gemini helps you let go of an idea that's no longer working for you. Finally, on Wednesday, Mars enters Virgo and helps you talk through a disagreement with care.

Get creative, Leo. Sagittarius season begins on Sunday, giving you a burst of inspiration that lasts all month. Put these vibes to good use and experiment artistically through painting, writing, photography, dancing, etc—whatever seems like fun. Tuesday's Full Moon in Gemini gives you an opportunity to catch up with your friends. Make some time for happy hour or at least a phone call. Finally, on Wednesday, Mars moves into Virgo and helps you get your budget in order in time for the holidays.

Remember your childhood, Virgo. The Sun moves into Sagittarius on Sunday, and for the next month, you're feeling nostalgic. Return to your favorite activities, books, movies, and songs from childhood, and of course, this is a good time to catch up with family or old friends, too. On Tuesday, the Full Moon in Gemini is a great opportunity to ask for a promotion or apply to a new job. Finally, on Wednesday, Mars enters your sign and gives you an extra boost of confidence that'll last til February.

Don't put your foot in your mouth, Libra. Sagittarius season begins on Sunday and makes you more outspoken for the next month—but you might accidentally say the wrong thing the wrong way. Tuesday's Full Moon in Gemini is the perfect time to share those photos or post that TikTok video from your last vacation. Then, on Wednesday, Mars enters Virgo and helps you cut someone or something off for good.

How's your budget, Scorpio? Sagittarius season begins on Sunday and puts the cosmic spotlight on your finances. You might be tempted to make impulse purchases right now, so keep a close eye on your spending habits. On Tuesday, a Full Moon in Gemini offers you a moment to make a change. Finally, on Wednesday, Mars moves into Virgo and helps you connect with your friends—but watch out for group chat drama over the next few months.

Happy birthday, Sagittarius! It's your season, so spend the next month celebrating you. You can also treat Sunday—the day the Sun moves into your sign—like a mini New Year. Tell the universe your resolutions. On Tuesday, a Full Moon in Gemini shines on your closest relationship, whether it's with a romantic partner or best friend. Spend some time with them and let them know how much you care. On Wednesday, Mars moves into Virgo and helps you approach your career with a renewed sense of focus and determination. Go get that promotion/new job/raise/bonus/etc.

Look inside, Capricorn. Sagittarius season begins on Sunday, and your season is next. That means that this month is a time for rest, release, and quiet introspection before you become the main character. Tuesday's Full Moon in Gemini gives you a realization about changes you can make to your daily routine. Finally, on Wednesday, Mars enters Virgo and helps you plan your next vacation—or go on it.

Need an excuse to throw a party, Aquarius? Beginning on Sunday, Sagittarius season wants you to focus on your friends, and not just your bestie, but your whole social circle. Friendsgiving, a holiday party, or just a regular old happy hour—make sure you have some fun socializing on your schedule this month. On Tuesday, a Full Moon in Gemini gives you a burst of creative inspiration. And on Wednesday, Mars slides into Virgo and powers your personal transformation.

Work hard, Pisces. Sagittarius begins on Sunday and for the next month, the cosmic spotlight is on your career. If you do well, you could be rewarded with a promotion, a raise, or a bonus. But if you don't, that'll be noticed, too. Tuesday's Full Moon in Gemini has you feeling nostalgic. Go ahead and revisit your favorite memories tonight. Finally, on Wednesday, Mars moves into Virgo. If you're partnered, you might be feeling an extra surge of desire for your partner. If you're not, this isn't a good time for casual hookups; instead, spend some quality (platonic) time with your best friend.

#6999. Senior Implementation Engineer at Clutch

Clutch helps credit unions launch digital lending and account opening. As a Senior Implementation Engineer, you're the product specialist who solves the hardest technical problems on those projects.

You specialize in one Clutch product and cross-train on a second. You join each project at key moments and whenever it needs something beyond the default setup. An Engagement Manager owns each project and the relationship with the credit union.

This role fits someone who can diagnose a complicated setup, explain it to a non-technical audience and decide when a request is worth building.

Diagnose complicated setups and find working answers through configuration, mapping, integration design and workarounds

Design and build non-standard configuration, core mapping and integrations

Decide whether a requested change is worth making. When it isn't, say no, explain why and offer an alternative

Own the testing strategy and lead live testing sessions with credit union teams

Advise our implementation team on what's possible, what needs a workaround and what's a true product gap. File clear tickets with our product and engineering teams for the gaps

Help write and improve our best-practice default setup, workbooks and test scripts with our Product and Engineering teams

Coach Engagement Managers to run standard setup themselves

Use AI to speed up your work, and standardize what works with the team

Work on many projects at once. Occasional onsite visits with customers.

Our Professional Services team implements Clutch for our credit union customers. Every project starts from a best-practice default, and we change it when the change improves the credit union's outcome. We test the setup with the credit union and take them live. Our top metric is customer success through renewal and expansion. We look for people with high agency, sound judgment and a strong technical foundation, who keep teammates informed.

Our company is growing quickly, and so is our team. We're looking for people who want to help shape a world-class delivery motion.

Learn our default setups, decision log and the core and vendor systems around your product

Join your first projects alongside a tenured Implementation Engineer

Complete onboarding and lead the technical design on several projects

Handle your first non-standard configurations and integrations on your own

Be the go-to person for your product area

Contribute to our default setups, workbooks and test scripts

Coach Engagement Managers on standard setup

5+ years of experience in implementation, solutions engineering or technical consulting, ideally in fintech or banking software

2+ years integrating with web APIs. You read and write code (Python, JSON, JavaScript or similar), work comfortably in GitHub and the terminal, and debug payloads yourself

A record of diagnosing complex setups and finding working answers without engineering work

Sound judgment on which changes are worth making, and the confidence to say no and explain why

A grasp of why a product works the way it does and what it does for a customer's business

A named result from using AI in your work, such as time saved or a faster diagnosis

Comfort with ambiguity. You build a plan, tracker or checklist when none exists

The ability to explain technical concepts clearly to non-technical audiences

You communicate proactively. You flag risks early and keep Engagement Managers and customers informed

The ability to keep many projects moving in the same week

Experience with core banking or credit union core systems, Alloy, Stripe, Plaid or ACH

Please note that this role may evolve as our business needs change, so we appreciate your flexibility and adaptability.

Remote Flexibility: Enjoy the freedom of remote work from anywhere, balancing life and career seamlessly.

Unforgettable Off-Sites: Twice a year, bond with colleagues in exciting destinations, fostering teamwork and fresh ideas.

Paid Time Off: Enjoy flexible PTO days yearly for relaxation and rejuvenation.

Stock Options: Joining us means having a stake in our success, so you'll receive stock options as part of your compensation package.

Home Office Setup: Create your ideal workspace with a dedicated budget for home office essentials.

Work Trip Budget: Grow personally and professionally with a budget for work-related trips and co-working.

Health Coverage: Prioritize your well-being with comprehensive health and dental insurance plans.

Clutch is a revolutionary vertical SaaS company, proudly backed by Andreessen Horowitz (A16z), aimed at revolutionizing the way Credit Unions engage and change the lives of their members. As a champion of financial well-being, we address the urgent need for affordable lending solutions in an era where the average American grapples with over $155,000 in household debt. Unlike traditional financial institutions, Clutch develops software to turn Credit Unions into FinTech lenders and leverage their balance sheets to responsibly lend to over 130M Americans. Our mission extends beyond mere financial transactions; we strive to fundamentally enhance the way credit unions interact with their members. By integrating cutting-edge technologies and user-centric designs, we help credit unions provide seamless digital experiences that are on par with leading tech companies. This approach not only preserves but revitalizes the longstanding tradition of community and member-focused service inherent to credit unions.

Clutch is an equal opportunity employer. All applicants will be considered for employment without attention to race, color, religion, sex, sexual orientation, gender identity, national origin, veteran or disability status.

Our organization participates in E-Verify. Click here to learn about E-Verify.A Note About AI at ClutchWe love AI. We use it often and encourage our team to creatively and effectively leverage AI tools in their work. If you join Clutch, we hope you'll bring the same enthusiasm for exploring how AI can amplify impact, productivity, and innovation.That said, during the interview process, we want to hear your thoughts. Please approach interviews without the use of AI tools—our goal is to get to know how you think, solve problems, and communicate. Once you're in the seat, bring on the prompts!

US Tier II: (All Other): $124K-$145KActual pay may vary based on qualifications, skills, experience, and location. Base salary does not include bonus, equity, or benefits, which are offered in addition as applicable.

Airbnb was born in 2007 when two hosts welcomed three guests to their San Francisco home, and has since grown to over 5 million hosts who have welcomed over 2 billion guest arrivals in almost every country across the globe. Every day, hosts offer unique stays and experiences that make it possible for guests to connect with communities in a more authentic way.

Airbnb’s mission is to create a world where anyone can belong anywhere. Essential to this mission is the ability to have authentic conversations with members of a global community. The Messaging Foundations team builds the platform that powers these conversations. As a Senior Software Engineer on the team, you will build innovative products, architect scalable solutions, collaborate across organizations, and help shape our roadmap - ensuring that our global community of guests and hosts can communicate seamlessly, wherever they are.

You will build and operate the reliable, performant, and scalable infrastructure that powers all messaging experiences across Airbnb. You will build a platform that enables product teams across Airbnb to innovate new conversational experiences faster. You will support and deliver AI-powered features as part of the messaging intelligence platform, driving improvements in trip quality and reducing host effort through contextual assistance.

This position is US - Remote Eligible. The role may include occasional work at an Airbnb office or attendance at offsites, as agreed to with your manager. While the position is Remote Eligible, you must live in a state where Airbnb, Inc. has a registered entity. Click here for the up-to-date list of excluded states. This list is continuously evolving, so please check back with us if the state you live in is on the exclusion list . We strive to also provide a disability inclusive application and interview process. If you are a candidate with a disability and require reasonable accommodation in order to submit an application, please contact us at: [email protected]. A Note on Recruiting Scams: Scammers sometimes pose as Airbnb recruiters to get money or personal information from candidates. A few things that will always be true for Airbnb’s hiring process: our open roles are posted on Airbnb’s Career’s Page at careers.airbnb.com, and our recruiters correspond only from @airbnb.com or @ext.airbnb.com email addresses. Our recruiters will never ask for your Social Security number, bank account details, passport, or payment app information while you are interviewing.  We’ll also never ask you to pay a fee, send money, deposit or cash a check, or purchase work-related equipment (such as a company laptop) during the interview process. We encourage candidates to remain vigilant of these recruiting scams and not share sensitive information if you do not believe an individual is actually affiliated with Airbnb.

#6995. 5 Ways Warmth Has Shaped Interior Design in 2026, According to Experts

Warmth is more than a palette—it’s the secret to a home you never want to leave.

After years of cool neutrals and minimalist interiors, there’s been a shift toward warmer, more inviting spaces that feel layered, personal, and lived-in.

“Warmth has become more than just a color palette or a particular material. It's about spaces that feel authentic, comfortable, and reflective of the people who live in them,” says Laura Gerber, an interior designer, project manager, and production coordinator with ODE Design.

At the end of the day, a warm home should feel inviting and effortless, with materials and details that make people want to spend time there. If you’re looking to add some warmth to your home, take inspiration from these designer-approved ideas.

Wood’s organic grain brings instant warmth to both modern and traditional homes, adding depth without making a space feel overly formal.

“We’re incorporating more white oak, walnut, and rift-cut cabinetry than we have in years, along with wood ceilings and paneled walls,” explains Rachel Simpson, senior interior designer at Revive Design and Renovation.

There’s been a shift from stark white countertops toward natural quartzite, travertine, and marble with warmer veining. These materials have natural variation and movement that bring character into a space.

“Honed finishes, in particular, feel softer and more organic than high-polish surfaces. They allow the beauty of the stone to speak for itself without feeling overly formal or glossy,” explains Simpson.

These days, people really want their homes to feel like a sanctuary.

“In a recent project, the client envisioned a grown-up treehouse nestled among the evergreens. We wanted to create a warm yet contemporary mountain retreat that felt luxurious but also comfortable and welcoming,” says Charles Greenfeld, founder and principal designer at Ethan Charles Design.

Rather than relying on one or two dramatic statement pieces, the interiors were designed to be experienced as a whole, with each detail contributing to that warm aesthetic.

Creating warmth is really about creating layers of thoughtfully curated design elements. This helps to create a home you want to enter and never leave.

“Well-proportioned rugs, textures on walls, loads of art, draperies, and lighting overhead and at eye level are just some of the things we can add to our spaces to make them feel cozy and accessible,” says Ashley Powell, cofounder and interior designer of Roseberry Allen.

Designers have lately been drawn to rooms where the colors are close to one another, but the materials do different things.

“A woven rug, a textured wall, and a piece of wood with a visible grain each catch the light differently,” says Jess Klein, founder and principal of Jess Klein Studio.

That variation adds visual depth and makes the room feel warmer and more inviting, even when the color palette is understated.

Follow House Beautiful on Instagram and TikTok.

#6985. The “Scottish Country House” Look Is the Cozy Interior Trend Designers Say to Know This Season

You don't need a Highland cottage to nail this heritage-inspired look.

Every item on this page was hand-picked by a House Beautiful editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

You don't need a sprawling stone cottage in the Highlands, a sweeping countryside view, or a roaring fireplace to bring a little Scottish country-house charm into your home. In fact, the appeal of this cozy, heritage-inspired aesthetic is that it can work just as beautifully in a city apartment as it does in a centuries-old estate.

The secret is mixing those old-world details with pieces that feel fresh and livable, so your home doesn't end up looking like a period drama set. A tartan runner on the dining table, a cashmere throw draped over your favorite reading chair, or a few leather accents on the coffee table can bring just the right amount of countryside character to an otherwise modern space. Strathberry's new tartan homeware collection is another lovely entry point, giving the traditional pattern a contemporary spin that feels right at home in all kinds of interiors.

Whether you're fully embracing your countryside daydreams or just looking to make your space feel a little warmer and more layered this winter, there are plenty of ways to bring the look home. Read on for the Scottish country-house-inspired touches that make this aesthetic so charming—and how to work them into your own space.

If there's one pattern that deserves a starring role in a Scottish country-inspired home, it's tartan. And you don't have to limit it to a throw blanket or a few accent pillows. Designer Kaitlin Smith recommends “using plaid for a wallpaper or even as an accent pillow” to bring that distinctly Scottish feel into a space.

For a bolder take, try a tartan wallpaper in a powder room, entryway, or tucked-away reading nook. If you'd rather ease into the look, a single plaid pillow or upholstered accent piece can make just as much of a statement. With so many different tartan patterns and colorways to choose from, you can find one that brings a little heritage-inspired character to your home without making the whole room feel like a theme.

A Scottish country house should feel like it's been lived in for generations, not styled within an inch of its life. That's where woven baskets come in.

Noel Gatts, a designer and HGTV personality, recommends introducing “some slouchy woven baskets” throughout the home, whether at the entryway for shoes and umbrellas or in a living room or bedroom to hold extra blankets. Their slightly relaxed shape and natural texture add to that collected, lived-in feeling while giving everyday clutter somewhere to go.

Try a larger basket beside the front door, a smaller one tucked under a console or an oversized version next to your favorite reading chair. It's a practical detail that also brings in the natural materials that make this aesthetic feel so grounded. Plus, having a cozy throw within arm's reach is never a bad idea.

You can practically picture a pot of tea warming by the fire in a Scottish country house, and Gatts recommends leaning into that feeling with a thoughtful arrangement of crockery and candles.

“Feature a fireplace or dining table with tea pots, earthenware, and an installation of candles and/or candlesticks,” she suggests. This is a lovely opportunity to bring out those pieces you might otherwise save for special occasions, from vintage teapots and handmade ceramic bowls to mismatched candlesticks and well-loved serving dishes. Grouping them together creates a charming focal point without making the space feel overly formal.

Candlelight is especially important here, adding a soft, welcoming glow that feels worlds away from bright overhead lighting. And if you don't have a fireplace, a dining table, mantel or console can give you the same cozy effect.

A Scottish country house shouldn't feel too coordinated, so don't be afraid to bring a few different patterns into the mix. As designer Sarah Cole suggests, “try pairing a wool plaid with a botanical print, a small geometric pattern or a traditional rug.”

The combination adds visual interest and gives a room that layered, lived-in quality that makes country-house interiors so inviting. You can keep the look cohesive by pulling a few warm, autumnal colors throughout the patterns—like russets, burgundies, forest greens and ochres—while varying their scale so they don't compete with one another. It's a simple way to make the space feel more personal and collected over time.

The Scottish country-house look has a wonderful appreciation for pieces that feel like they've been around awhile. Rather than filling every surface with brand-new accessories, Gatts suggests bringing in “found objects with a sense of history” made from weathered wood, pottery, or patinated brass. These are the kinds of details that give a room its personality, whether it's an old wooden box on a coffee table, a vintage ceramic vessel or a brass object with a little wear and patina.

Don't worry about everything matching, either. A mix of materials and finishes makes the space feel more authentic and less like you ordered the entire room in one shopping trip. Gatts recommends using these pieces on a coffee table for “the perfect antique flair,” but a bookshelf, sideboard, or entryway console would work just as well.

Finally, a few well-chosen antique details can give a room that Highland lodge feeling without requiring a full redesign. Jill Wheeler of Birch Hill Interiors recommends adding “a touch of polished brass like antique candlesticks or wall sconces to give the space a refined look.” Try styling a pair of vintage brass candlesticks on a mantel, placing one on a coffee table, or swapping in brass wall sconces beside a favorite reading chair.

Wheeler also suggests incorporating antiques on a mantel, coffee table, or built-in, noting that “antiques can help bring the outside in and tell a story.” The mix of aged finishes and meaningful finds adds a sense of history and character that makes a Scottish country-inspired space feel genuinely lived in.

Follow House Beautiful on Instagram and TikTok.

#6982. Storage Platform Engineer at Supabase

Supabase runs millions of Postgres databases, and storage performance is now one of the biggest levers on how fast, reliable, and affordable they are. We're building a software-defined, NVMe-over-Fabrics storage platform underneath Supabase Postgres. Postgres stays unmodified, and the new layer delivers near-local-NVMe performance with the durability and flexibility of networked storage.

As the senior engineer driving storage, you'll own this effort end to end: the architecture and build-vs-buy decisions, integration into our compute platform, and operating the system in production.

Your first goal is raw Postgres I/O performance at scale. In the longer term, the layer you build will provide the block-level snapshots and copy-on-write clones behind database branching, fast restores, and instant resizes. Because it's software-defined, it will also travel with us as we expand beyond a single cloud provider into multi-cloud and colocated infrastructure.

This is a foundational, high-ownership role. You'll set the technical direction for storage at Supabase and help grow the team around it.

Lead the architecture of Supabase's NVMe-oF storage platform, including evaluating and deciding between adopting existing software-defined storage and building on SPDK

Integrate networked block storage into our Kubernetes-based compute platform through CSI drivers and custom controllers, covering provisioning, attach/detach, resizing, and migration

Make Postgres fast on networked storage by profiling and tuning the full I/O path, from fsync, WAL, and checkpoints through the kernel block layer, NVMe/TCP, and the network

Design for durability and availability, including replication or erasure coding, failure domains, failover, and data-integrity verification

Build I/O isolation and QoS so that noisy neighbors can't degrade other tenants

Set performance and reliability SLOs, and build the benchmarking and observability to hold the system to them

Design the layer for block-level snapshots and copy-on-write clones, laying the groundwork for database branching

Keep the platform portable across AWS, other clouds, and bare-metal/colo

Write design docs, mentor engineers, and work closely with the Compute, Branching, Postgres, and Multicloud teams

Participate in on-call rotations, incident response, and post-incident reviews

Resolve escalated support issues, while also identifying and resolving systemic issues across the platform

Have 7+ years of software engineering experience, including significant time building and operating storage systems in production

Have built or operated distributed or software-defined block storage (for example Ceph RBD, Lightbits, Simplyblock, Portworx, LINSTOR/DRBD, or an in-house system)

Have hands-on experience with NVMe/TCP or NVMe-oF, including targets, initiators, multipath, and failover behavior

Have a deep understanding of Linux storage internals: the block layer, io_uring, page cache, O_DIRECT and fsync semantics, filesystems, and device-mapper

Write strong systems code in Go, plus C, C++, or Rust

Measure performance rigorously with fio, pgbench, eBPF, and perf, and can explain latency down to its individual components

Understand durability tradeoffs between replication, erasure coding, and consistency under failure

Have led technical direction on a complex system: design docs, tradeoffs, and bringing others along

Have experience with Kubernetes controllers and/or CSI drivers

Have been on call for large production systems

Communicate clearly in writing and thrive in async, globally distributed teams

Experience implementing block-level snapshots, thin provisioning, or copy-on-write cloning

Postgres internals knowledge (WAL, buffer manager, checkpointer), or familiarity with OrioleDB

Experience with AWS networking and instance storage at scale (ENA, placement, bandwidth limits)

Experience running storage on bare metal or in colo

Contributions to open source storage projects or the Linux kernel storage stack

#6978. Software Engineer - Postgres Tooling at Supabase

Supabase is the Postgres development platform, built by developers for developers. We provide a complete backend solution including Database, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, and Vector Search. All services are deeply integrated and designed for growth.

We’re looking for a Software Engineer, Postgres Tooling to join our CLI team and help build the open-source tools developers use to change their Postgres schemas safely. You’ll join as a co-maintainer of pg-delta, our schema diff and migration engine, and grow into leading it.

Given two databases, or a database and a folder of SQL files describing the schema you want, pg-delta works out the differences and generates the migration to get from one to the other. It powers supabase db diff, db pull, and declarative schemas, and it runs underneath Supabase’s platform, branching, and AI agent workflows. Every migration it generates is verified on a throwaway clone before it’s trusted, and it’s tested against 331 scenarios on PostgreSQL 14–18. We’re now working toward a stable, standalone 1.0 release for any Postgres developer, on Supabase or not.

This is a correctness-first role. The SQL this tool generates runs against production databases, so privilege safety and avoiding data loss are non-negotiable. It’s a great fit for someone who loves Postgres at the catalog level, enjoys compiler-style problems, and thrives in async, fast-paced environments.

Co-maintain pg-delta: review and merge changes, including PRs written by AI agents, and grow into its lead maintainer.

Own correctness: triage bug reports from GitHub and support, fix diff and dependency-ordering bugs, and extend test coverage across Postgres versions.

Make sure generated migrations are privilege-safe and never silently lose data, by detecting destructive changes and rewriting them into safe equivalents where possible.

Help take pg-delta to a stable 1.0: a public API with a semver contract, documentation, and broader coverage of Postgres extensions.

Work with the open-source community: triage issues, review outside contributions, and say no gracefully when a request doesn’t fit.

Work with the teams across Supabase that build on pg-delta, so migrations are generated and run the same way everywhere.

Write the design docs and technical strategy that set the tool’s direction, working async across teams.

Share the CLI team’s on-call rotation. Weekdays are covered follow-the-sun, and because we ship packaged software that users can roll back, pages are rare.

Have 8+ years of experience as a software engineer, including deep, hands-on work with PostgreSQL.

Know Postgres internals at the catalog level: pg_catalog, pg_depend, object ownership, grants and default privileges, RLS policies, and extension membership.

Understand DDL and migration semantics: which changes take locks, which can’t run inside a transaction, how dependencies determine ordering, and how to spot a destructive change.

Treat a migration that silently loosens access as a top-severity bug.

Write strong TypeScript, and design tests for correctness, such as differential, property-based, or corpus testing across multiple Postgres versions.

Are comfortable with compiler-style problems: dependency graphs, topological ordering, cycle handling, and diffing.

Can confidently review complex changes in an intricate codebase, including AI-generated PRs.

Have owned software in production with users in the loop: staged rollouts, error monitoring, and triaging real bug reports.

Have led a technical project across several teams, from an ambiguous start to production.

Communicate clearly in writing and thrive in async, globally distributed teams.

Nice to have, roughly in order of how much they’d help:

Have built or maintained schema diff or migration tooling (such as migra, pg-schema-diff, Atlas, pgroll, sqitch, Prisma Migrate, or Flyway/Liquibase), or worked with pg_dump internals.

Open-source maintainer experience: API contracts, semver, documentation, and community triage.

Familiarity with Postgres extensions such as pg_cron, pgmq, pg_partman, vault, pg_net, pgvector, or PostGIS.

Contributions to Postgres itself, or authorship of a Postgres extension.

Experience with Supabase or another managed Postgres platform.

#6960. The Beyond Devastating ‘Sunrise on the Reaping’ Ending, Explained

Crying too hard at the end of The Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping to fully grasp those final scenes? It can happen to the best of us. Let’s break down the ending of Sunrise on the Reaping to make sure you didn’t miss anything, including some of the things that Suzanne Collins’ book explains in a tiiiiiny bit more detail.

It should come as no surprise that Haymitch wins the Hunger Games. You should know that going in, even if you haven’t read Sunrise on the Reaping. When we first met the character in The Hunger Games, he was Katniss and Peeta’s mentor because he was District 12’s only living victor.

What we didn’t totally know before the release of this book, and subsequent film, was that Haymitch teamed up with other recent victors like Beetee, Mags, and Wiress (as well as future gamemaker Plutarch Heavensbee) to try and take down the games from within. As we first learned that in Catching Fire, the flaws that Haymitch found in the arena contributed to his victory.

President Snow does not like to be beaten and finds creative ways to get revenge against those who double-cross him. We see that early on with Beetee’s son Ampert, who gets reaped to punish Beetee for how he won his games. We see it later with Peeta and Katniss. And Haymitch is allowed to return home at the expense of his mother, brother, and girlfriend. All three die horrifically as soon as he gets back. As many have said: Haymitch didn’t drink enough.

At the end of the film, we see how President Snow’s revenge against Haymitch’s community causes him to spiral fast. With his family and girlfriend dead, in addition to the PTSD from surviving the Hunger Games, he becomes inconsolable and addicted to sleeping syrup. It does not help that he is the only person living in Victor’s Village; because District 12’s only other victor, Lucy Gray Baird, went missing 40 years prior.

One day, Plutarch comes to visit Haymitch ahead of his victory tour to film an update for the Capitol News, and they have a chat. (In the book, this conversation happens during the tour.) He wants Haymitch to keep working with them to overthrow the Capitol. He refuses. “I am living proof the Capitol always wins,” Haymitch says. “You need someone different from me.”

Plutarch insists that they need someone exactly like him, actually. Even if it takes generations and they don’t get to see the results, they have to keep imagining a different future so that one day it comes true. It doesn’t make them heroes. It doesn’t even make them good people. But they’re trying.

Haymitch drifts away from two of his only friends, Burdock Everdeen and Asterid March. If you couldn’t tell from their names, or didn’t hear what they call their infant daughter, yes! Those are Katniss’ parents. (Also, yes! Burdock Everdeen is a member of the Covey. That’s why he sings one of Lucy Gray’s songs, "The Old Therebefore," at the funeral for District 12’s tributes. One day, he’ll change the course of history by teaching her “The Hanging Tree.”

The main reason that Haymitch cuts himself off is grief and guilt. He wants to be alone with his pain. Losing the people he was closest too makes him feel like he doesn’t deserve to be close to anyone, and maybe shouldn’t for their own protection. Movie protagonists are always thinking that, aren’t they? Like, okay Spider-Man! Whatever you say!

But the book reveals another, even more devastating reason: when baby Katniss grows up and starts wearing her hair in pigtail braids, she looks just like Haymitch’s “sweetheart” and first ally, Louella.

As we know from reading and watching The Hunger Games, the rebellion in Panem did eventually find the right victors to raise an army and end the games for good in District 12’s own Peeta Mellark and Katniss Everdeen. They were able to finally implement the plans that Beetee, Wiress, and the other victors had been testing since the second Quarter Quell, if not before.

Without even knowing it, Peeta and Katniss helped Haymitch keep his promise to Lenore Dove. So, of course, the story has to end with them. We see Haymitch, again played by Woody Harrelson, finally celebrating his birthday with no reapings. In Collin’s novel, Haymitch tells Katniss and Peeta about Lenore Dove in order to add her to a District 12 book of memories they’re putting together. Katniss gifts him goose eggs to hatch and raise. The film, however, takes a simpler approach, and just shows old man Haymitch greeting his former mentees before going to spend some alone time with the geese. He’s finally earned some peace.

#6952. Are We in a Sugar Baby Economy?

Dating is expensive, so why not get paid for it?

Ana, 37, has a rule: she doesn't spend her own money on men. Not on dinner, not on an Uber home, and definitely not on any emotional labor of a situationship that she knows isn’t going to go anywhere. She’s had this rule for three years now.

“I realized over the years that dating men came with a lot of risk,” she tells Cosmopolitan. Ana is bisexual, and she says she kept running into the same thing with men that she never felt with women: a lack of transparency, and the creeping sense of being used.

“At the end of the day, it seemed like the majority of the men I had dated wanted an embellished sex worker, who also provided care and mothering, without necessarily providing something equivalent back.” So she decided to make sure she was getting something back. “Because money, I can count,” she says. “Fake promises? Not really.”

Dating in 2026 is already an expensive extra-curricular on its own. BMO's research puts the average Gen Z date at around $205 once you add up the outfit, the ride, and the actual dinner (millennials clock in even higher, at $252). The average all-in cost of a date climbed 12.5 percent to $189, outpacing the cost of living itself. Bank of America found that more than half of Gen Z, 53 percent of men and 54 percent of women, spend literally $0 a month on dating—because they’re just not doing it.

But even when people are willing to pay the price, it’s hard to find someone to splurge on. Dating app sessions dropped 7 percent year over year in 2025. Swiping is tiring, and paying for premium tiers to keep seeing the same guy you’ve swiped left on on four other apps doesn’t seem worth it. So when “sugar baby” discourse started taking over TikTok and the timeline, it wasn't random.

Clu, 29, remembers exactly how it started. She was working a day job, hustling for what seemed like every dollar, and watching friends who were sugar babies get everything paid for.

“It started off as a financial pressure,” she says. “I'm looking around, I'm seeing these girls get everything paid for, and a lot of my friends are also sugar babies. They're like, ‘Girl, just do it.’”

The tipping point was dating guys her own age and not getting the same treatment. Men who were older and financially stable, on the other hand, knew exactly what they wanted—and were willing to pay for it.

What she's selling is mostly time. “It's really just companionship,” she says, “Going on dates and pretending, most of the time.” Some men want a hand to hold at a lake after a rough day at work while others want a full-on girlfriend experience and promise to cover everything from her bills to a new car. The pay varies wildly: a couple hundred dollars for a date, or, if you get lucky, a designer bag. Twice, Clu says, married men have told her their wives knew and had given their blessing, because they couldn't give their husbands what they were looking for.

“You always want to think of it as a financial transaction because this is a service,” she says. “I'm providing you a service of having a good time and escaping from your reality." Catch feelings, she warns, and things crumble. And never let yourself believe you're the prize: "There's always someone younger. There's always gonna be another sugar baby."

And at first, Clu says the money felt like freedom, but eventually that feeling faded.

“After a while, it does take a toll, because you're constantly depending on these people,” she says. “They can stop paying your bills tomorrow. So it does become financial survival.”

A 2024 study of 77 people in the U.S. and Canada found that money was the primary driver for sugar babies, but companionship mattered to everyone involved, and plenty of arrangements looked a lot like regular dating. It also found that more than a third of the sugar babies had done other kinds of transactional sex work, and that they worried about physical safety and coercion, especially early on.

Sugar dating itself isn't illegal in most places, according to legal explainers on the subject. The line tends to come down to whether money is being exchanged specifically for sex. Which is exactly why so many arrangements stay carefully, deliberately vague.

Ana's current setup doesn't fit the stereotype at all. There's no monthly allowance. Her partner covers things like groceries and Ubers and generally makes her life easier. They live separately and see each other once a week: date night on Saturday, an overnight, brunch on Sunday. They text daily. They aren't exclusive. And all of it was spelled out from the start. “Those were the conditions I set,” she says. “I no longer engage with men without some kind of arrangement being set from the start.”

Before this, she had two more traditional sugar-daddy relationships, which worked more like pay-per-meet. Roughly twice a month, she'd ask for a specific bill to be paid: a shopping spree, repairs at her house, a doctor's bill. She has never liked the idea of a monthly allowance, she explains, because she worried it would make her feel obligated to see people even when she didn't want to.

That autonomy is the point. Ana doesn't frame this as freedom (”I already had that before,” she says) so much as respect. “I have never been treated this nice by men whom I gave my time and love and care for free, so that says a lot.”

“All relationship types come with compromises. But most relationships between men and women come with sometimes feeling pressured to do stuff. And I have learned not to bow.”

But Ana says the sites themselves have changed. "Most sugar arrangement sites have become a place for medium income men to underpay for sex work," she says. In her experience, a lot of them aren't looking for anything complex. “They do want sex by the hour but say they don't want to use escorts as they feel bad about themselves.”

James Foley, LCSW-R, a psychotherapist, treats men who engage in what he calls sugaring behaviors, and he says the economy and the internet both play a role. He explains that social media has given everyone a 24/7, high-resolution view of the rich: their cars, homes, and vacations. Technology, meanwhile, lets people at opposite ends of the economic spectrum connect discreetly and make deals.

But both people—sugar daddies and babies alike–—are walking into an emotionally powerful situation they sometimes can't manage.

“It is a tremendous struggle to maintain your reality testing when something is real and fake at the same time,” he says.

“If you come across the opportunity, why not take it?" Clu says. “Go on that date and come back with that Cartier.” She encourages women to take the chance, as long as they build real relationships instead of treating each man as a one-time payout.

Ana, on the other hand, isn't going back to traditional dating in any form.

In 2026, a lot of people feel like they're paying more and getting less, for dinner, for rent, for love. Sugar dating promises to flip that equation, whether it actually does depends on the contract, the person across the table, and how long you can keep the line between transaction and relationship from blurring.

As Clu puts it, you can be confident in what these men will do for you, and still know the balance can change overnight. Anyone considering it should go in knowing that.
